## Volume 8 Batch 0001 (ch 421–434) — AN ARRIVAL

**Volume 8 opens on the page. Fourteen chapters, day 1,091 to day 1,152, 27,657 words of body prose, average ~1,976.
One bracketed layer line in the whole batch, at ch 434. Batch irreversible step at ch 432. Midpoint at ch 427.
Physical-risk card at ch 430.**

- **421 — The Eighteenth, And Nothing Else.** Days 1,091–1,092, the twenty-eighth and the twenty-ninth. **The chapter now runs strictly forward, and the twenty-ninth is shared with ch 422: she is at the rail at the sixth hour of the twenty-ninth and is at the mill yard at the ninth hour of it.** The rail at the sixth hour with forty-nine
  lines and then fifty-one, every one measured by the weight or by the length, and she can take none. **Eleven minutes
  at that rail and nothing happened and nothing was asked of her** — and she works out the calendar: the ground asks
  her a question on the fourth, the eleventh, the eighteenth and the twenty-fourth, and today is not one of the four. A
  cup of something hot at the yard at the turn that her hand will not take and that she watched go cold. The second
  hour: **the six words, once, and again, and they sound the same.** She wants the word at the head of the strip
  against her ribs and has decided, on the nineteenth of a month thirty-nine days back, in a room with the door shut, that
  she would rather have the standing arrangement than the line, and she still has that. **And at the seventh hour of the evening a
  cart she does not know comes down the mill road and does not stop at the gate and does not stop at the counting house,
  with a tray on it and a cord round it, and the man on it does not get out.**
- **422 — The Woman Who Came On Foot.** Days 1,092–1,094, **opening on the twenty-ninth at the ninth hour, after ch 421's sixth hour.** The cart is standing in the mill yard with its near wheel
  sunk four inches and a strip under a stone on the seat board **with one date on it and nothing else**, in the
  ordinary hand that comes about nine times a year. **She does not take it and does not wake him.** The mechanism she
  worked out herself at ch 366, said once, in short sentences, at the boards: a tray goes up on the first, comes down
  on the ninth with the empty trays, and that cart does not go east, so a strip that goes east does not come back on
  it; and something came back on a cart that does not go east. She says it four times and gets the same answer four
  times. She goes past the post on the mill road and does not stop, and does not go up the hill, and says both of those
  out loud. **And at the seventh hour of the first a woman of sixty-one gets off that cart at the mill, which is eleven
  miles from where she was picked up, and walks the four miles, and asks the keeper of nothing for a woman of
  eighteen who reads.**
- **423 — Nobody Has Ever Answered One.** Days 1,095–1,098, the seventh hour, in the open doorway with the boards
  showing. **Xie Changyin**, sixty-one, no folder, no men, no seal, no office. **She quotes the two lines and the date
  and the ordinary voice — a line has not been carried to a room it was made out for, and a holder is required against
  it — and the nineteenth, forty-three days back, and she does not know there is a man.** Ning Yan's first act
  is neither fear nor anger: she works out that the only way to answer correctly is to tell the truth, and that the
  truth is a thing she has never said out loud, and that saying it puts a voice in a stranger's book. **She says
  nothing. She does not lie.** Then she says no out loud, because the alternative is a stranger carrying a sentence
  east in her coat. **He is asked, for the first time, and he cannot answer, and it goes in no book at all.** And at
  the end she is offered one question, one, at a time of her choosing. **She did not come eleven miles to ask a
  question**, and she puts the folded sheet on the boards and does not open it.
- **424 — The Four Hundred Times.** Days 1,099–1,102. A carter of about thirty-four at a cart stand with a whip folded
  twice. **The structural answer, mouth one of six, in four or five short sentences broken across beats:** there is a
  place at the east end of that road where strips go and they do not come back · a journey with nothing on it is not a
  journey, and he was paid for the going and not for the coming back · turning back is the ordinary end of that
  journey and people are paid for it. **Four hundred and about forty turnings, counted on his own fingers, and the
  gate man has counted more.** Twenty-nine for a man with nothing on him, and no difference either way. **The eight
  coppers she is owed come up here and are not paid**, because she cannot take them and cannot give them and the road
  east is a trade. He asks her nothing. And she shouts one question back down the road, which she does not do, and
  learns that nobody who walks in from the far side of the turn has ever walked out again, and that it is not a thing
  anybody at that stand thinks about.
- **425 — A Rate And A Room And A Roll.** Days 1,103–1,106, the seventh hour, four days running, the folded sheet
  still unopened on the boards. **The offer in full and every term reasonable:** a long low lime-washed building eleven
  miles east, one storey, a hundred and forty feet front, a counter, **a lamp on every table**, about a hundred people
  reading, **no hall in it and nothing in it that is called a hall** · two coppers a page, a second rate by the
  difficulty of the hand, and a third at twice the first · **a licence that is not a piece of paper that says what you
  are** · a room, nine feet by eleven, four of them and three empty for eleven years · **standing** · **a book with
  four hundred and six names on it, and a name on it is a person who cannot be un-recruited.** **The trade is called
  the Order of the Recruited**, once, and the naming is the event. Her argument, once, in her own mouth, **and it is not
  wrong**: *you have been a reader for two years out of a shed for nothing and it has already taken a finger and half
  an arm out of you; be one properly and I will put the list of what it costs in front of you in a column and you can
  argue with a number instead of with a feeling.* Nine years ago she offered it to a woman in Ning Yan's position who
  took it and is alive and she has not spoken to her in six years. **She asks for nothing and buys nothing and does not
  mention the fine hand and does not mention the strip.** And at the door: what happens to a person who says no.
  **Nothing.** Two hundred recruited, about forty refusals, one single consequence for any of them.
- **426 — The Question She Had Not Thought To Ask.** Days 1,107–1,111. She works the order of the trade out of her own
  arithmetic, with nobody, because a reader is called in when a hand cannot be read. **Four people have called her in
  four times in a month and not one of them knew they were making a call.** Four hundred and six names in a book, and
  one of them has something in it that says a woman at a gate on that ground can read a hand nobody else on it can
  read, **and somebody worked that out before she did.** The five lines: a hand cannot be read · somebody has to find
  a person who can read it · that person says what it says, out loud, in the ordinary voice · that goes into a book ·
  and somebody, at some point, has to be a name at the head of a line. **The room is the reason, not the answer and not
  the refusal.** And for four hours she feels something she has not felt in two years and does not have a word for, and
  it is not fear and it is not hope. On the eighteenth she asks for one thing about one person and not one thing about
  herself, **and Xie Changyin says that is allowed and that it will be done in a room with a lamp in it.**
- **427 — The Sheet At The End Of The Arm.** Days 1,112–1,115. **The lifting floor**: a hundred feet, nine men, a bench
  in oak four inches thick, a chain, eleven dressed stones. **She asks for the lifting floor's own work to be read by
  somebody qualified** — not her own line, not the strip, not the fine hand. A young man with a good eye and no
  standing stands at the bench for two hours and eleven minutes in the order a floor reads a sheet in, and **finds
  nothing wrong in any of it and says so out loud, in a room with nine men in it, in the ordinary voice**, which is the
  first time in nineteen years that floor has been vouched by anybody not standing on it. **Geng writes his name at the
  foot of the sheet in his own hand, because that is what a floor does with a name, and does not thank anybody.**
  The dressed stone is still four feet off its frame and is not going back and nobody offers. She spent the only
  question she had on a stranger and got a stranger's good name into a room of nine men **and she does not know what to
  do with her face and she leaves the room.** At the door, Geng: the floor was a block short before she came and is two
  blocks short now, and nobody has worked out how to write that down.
- **428 — What He Has Watched Twice.** Days 1,116–1,119. The made road east of the turn at the ninth hour, nine feet
  off, notebook shut. **She asks one question: can a thing that has been read by somebody else be read back by the
  person it is about. Yes. It is not usually a good idea. He has watched it happen twice.** He does not say who and
  she does not ask twice and that is the whole of that page. He buys nothing, has never bought anything, and tells her
  that she has never once asked him twice about anything in seven months and that he has noticed it. Then the third
  floor at the fourth hour: **nine columns read out loud and all nine right and Rao says nothing about it, because she
  has said nothing for sixteen years.** The man with the brush asks about the slip for the fourth time and she says
  yes. The margin correction is four. And on the way back, **a man at the north end of a yard has asked four men a
  question about her out loud and has told nobody what the answer was.**
- **429 — The Eight Coppers She Is Owed.** Days 1,120–1,124. **Wei Ran**, twenty, one team, a whip folded twice.
  Eight coppers for a person with no load and nine for a load, said without looking at her. **She has twenty-one
  coppers and cannot be paid and is owed eight that her hand will not take, having stopped in the air over four of
  them twice on a plank bench.** **The rate is more than she has, because she has nothing she can be given.** Wei Ran
  takes the eight as a credit against the fare, writes it off a line, says the number out loud so she can argue with
  it, and says the balance of a fare can never be paid. **It is a trade and nothing in it is anything else.** She also
  says she has carried a man east once before and he did not come back. Then eleven days, written out on the boards in
  a column: the lifting floor, nine men, a sheet with two columns on it, the only thing on nine days of road that has
  ever made use of her, and **nobody to be told why.** **She goes east at the sixth hour of the second with a bar she
  cannot hold at the heavy end, and the made road goes four miles and then it does not.**
- **430 — The Man With The Chain.** Days 1,125–1,128. **The physical-risk card.** Eleven miles in two days on one arm at
  half and four fingers on one hand, buying every drop of water, and at the seventh mile **there is kerb on the road**,
  and nothing announces it. At the end of the long road there is a gate with two posts and a chain and a man with a
  cup and a stool, drinking cold tea at a carter. **The chain is across. Turn round.** Not because of anything she did,
  and he tells her the word before he says it and says she can come back in the morning and he will tell her all of
  it. **She goes back four hundred yards and stands in the road until the light goes and sleeps with a bar over her
  shoulders.** On the fourth at the sixth hour the chain is up and **he does not ask her a question, does not write
  anything down, and does not say one word about the first day to anybody.** A leg stops taking an order at a mile and a
  half and she gets it back at the second hour by standing on the grip of a bar in the ground. **She is on the far
  side of a maintained line for the first time and nothing announces that either.** And at two hundred yards: a long
  low lime-washed building, a lamp in every window, about a hundred people reading. **It is warm. She has eleven
  coppers. She is not hungry and has not been since the first Foundation. She does not go in, and not one of the
  hundred people inside has looked up.**
- **431 — The Second Attempt.** Days 1,129–1,133. The made road back at the second hour, then the shed at night. **She
  lays out eight people who have spoken to her about this and every one of them was right, and there is not one word
  written against any of them, and she has no counter-evidence of any kind.** Then the fifth thing she has taken
  apart in two years finds a place where it does not add up, and it is not a place where somebody was wrong: **it is
  a count.** Four calls in a month by people who did not know they were making them, and four hundred and six names in
  a book, and somebody has already written her down. **She cannot find the price out without saying yes and she is not
  going to say yes in order to find out what yes costs.** The hedge east of the turn, third listing, cut and checked and
  rubbed off: **six days and two hours**, said twice at the boards, the price said out loud first, **spent out of the
  ground under her and not out of her hands**, and the ring does not come up at all for two days. She says the six
  words out loud twice and they sound the same. And she stands up at the fourth hour and says *I am going to go* and
  *I want it written down*, and does not say the second one, and waits for the morning.
- **432 — The Date She Said Out Loud.** Days 1,134–1,141. **The batch's irreversible step, at the seventh hour of the
  eighteenth, in the doorway, in daylight.** Seven days of not saying it, the month written out on the boards, the four
  days of the ring. She lays out the three times she has done this before — a trestle on a bank, a gate, a counter —
  and what each one cost her. **And she says a date eleven days out and a place and her own agreement to both: the
  twenty-ninth, the sixth hour, the gate at the east end of the long road.** She asks for it to be written down and
  Xie Changyin writes a place, a day and an hour on the bottom of her own sheet **in the ordinary hand, and does not
  read it out, and says nothing at all.** Her name is not in it. Nothing was refused, nothing failed, no book will
  not shut, and **there is nobody she can go to and ask to take it out.** She gave up knowing what the fine hand says
  about her on the nineteenth of a month eighty-nine days back so that she would never be entered again, and she has
  been entered, and **the two events are eighty-nine days apart and nothing caused the second one.** And at the second hour
  she tells him, because she has never once decided a thing and not said it out loud to him at the boards.
- **433 — What He Did Not Say.** Days 1,142–1,144, and the scene is the second hour of the twenty-first. Two long days
  with nothing to say, and then **eleven times to the boards expecting to be argued with and argued with twice and four
  replies ready and not one of them used.** What he says is short and in the present tense: *I heard her at the seventh
  hour of the second day and I have been waiting three days for you to say it out loud.* Then **he tells her the
  price before she asks**, out of the ground under her, four days and six hours, and she notices he is doing it and
  asks why, and he says it is because she is going to ask in four seconds. **Then he says no**, and gives ch 419's
  reason in three sentences and says he is not going to argue it again and is telling her so now so she will not have
  to spend a morning in four months working out whether he has changed his mind. **She works out that she would have
  gone anyway, and that there are two versions of the week and she picked one on purpose in a doorway in daylight.**
  And the ring comes up a finger's width for the first time in three weeks, and something behind her fourth rib comes
  open, and **her short breath comes back to four inside a loaded reach for the first time since a dressed stone went
  the length of her own arm in front of nine men.** Ch 434 opens on the twenty-second.
- **434 — A Body That Cannot Stop.** Days 1,145–1,152. Seventy-six days of furnace and forty-nine of them with nothing in
  the shed to put a hand on, and a board that cannot pay her and a hand that cannot be given anything, and **so the only
  way to a stage is a person standing in the shed with her, and there is nobody she is entitled to ask.** Xie Changyin
  comes up the four miles on the twenty-second with a coil of rope, because there is nowhere on that road for her to be
  on the twenty-ninth except outside it, and says she has read one chapter on a furnace, can hold a rim with two hands,
  and is not going to pretend to be anything else. **It goes wrong once, the way it went wrong at ch 371, and nobody
  is burned.** She says so out loud four times, and Xie Changyin says that in forty-two years, at about nine in the
  morning, nobody has ever said the other one. [Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage eight, is held.] **The cost, named once, in her own thought: she cannot call a halt — not the arm, not the hand, not the four fingers, but herself, once a thing she is in has started.** And then it bites: she goes to the made road at the ninth hour and works out **four ways** to tell Xie Changyin she is not coming, **and all four of them work, and she does not use one, because she cannot.** She stands there and says *I am not going*, and it is true, and it goes nowhere, **and nothing in the world fails and nothing is written and no book will not shut.** She pays the stage, finds out what she paid it with, and **walks eleven miles east at the sixth hour of the twenty-ninth because she said a date out loud in daylight to a stranger and a date out loud is a date.** She is through the turnstile by the second hour. There is a long low lime-washed building with a lamp in every window and about a hundred people reading. **It is warm, and not one of them has looked up.**
- **435 — Four Sentences At The Gate.** Day 1,153, the thirtieth. Two days standing where people waiting stand, asking nothing, entering nothing. One question — do people waiting wait or go up — answered in four sentences: some stand; some go up; the chain does not tell them which; he does not call after them either way. She stays, counts bread, meets Xie Changyin passing, sleeps at the gate.
- **436 — The Road With Kerb On It.** Days 1,154–1,157, the 1st to the 4th. Walks the road east, studies seventh-mile kerb and twenty-one lamps, counts oil cost against eleven coppers. Past the counter: plain wood, man with brush, hundred readers, warm air. Told to come back on the second day for her question. Walks out without running.
- **437 — Forty Hands A Month.** Days 1,158–1,161, the 5th to the 8th. Mouth two of six: reader of twenty-six, forty hands a month, at his desk, from the far side of a counter, person never in the room, right every time, never told being right is the only thing keeping him there — broken across beats, never a speech. She watches morning sheets, eats with carriers, leaves him wiping his brush.
- **438 — One Question About One Person.** Days 1,162–1,166, the 9th to the 13th. Chooses herself: how Xie Changyin was recruited at nineteen out of a twenty-house village, sheet read aloud in a lamp room, yes, forty-two years, mother buried in the second year. About the person in the room, no third person needed. Answered in the ordinary voice; goes nowhere, and both know it.
- **439 — The Room That Reads.** Days 1,167–1,170, the 14th to the 17th. Learns the room inside: bundles, squaring, lamps at the sixth hour, hundred readers. First thing wrong: sheet with two hands, four failures, tied aside, work going on round it. She sees the fine lower half from nine feet, says not today, carries on.
- **440 — The Question He Would Not Answer.** Days 1,171–1,174, the 18th to the 21st. Hunter nine feet off the made road east of the turn, notebook shut, second and last page in Volume 8, she asks both times. Whether an institution that stops reading a person has done something to them: no; then unread four days is harder to find than read four years; first question anybody ever asked him he would rather not answer. He does not say who.
- **441 — Eleven Miles On Foot.** Days 1,175–1,179, the 22nd to the 26th. West to the shed, roof cord, boards; west five miles for bread, four loaves for eight; east again. Meets Wei Ran with team and cart — the batch's one Wei Ran card: eight-copper fare held as credit her hand will not take, opinion that the building is good, competent, honest, and will take her all the same. Walks the kerb mile, through at the fourth hour.
- **442 — Turned Back At The Chain.** Days 1,180–1,183, the 27th to the 30th. Chain across for timber taking the whole road, four hundred yards, two hours, not for anything she did. Crosses at the eighth hour, nothing written. Water at seven miles for a copper; leg stops at a mile and a half, bar-grip nine seconds. Learns to shut her mouth on answering.
- **443 — What He Decided In His Second Year.** Days 1,184–1,187, the 1st to the 4th. Mouth three of six at the same turnstile, once: his rule does not tell him what to do with people turned back; decided in his second year on his own; not saying what; not asked twice. She keeps the condition, carries bundles, watches the tied sheet wait.
- **444 — Inside The Counter.** Days 1,188–1,191, the 5th to the 8th. Carries inside the counter: one storey, boards, lamps, hundred readers, one book, one rate, one rule, no hall, no department, floor, rank list, committee, wing or title beyond recruited. Reads the rate board twice: two a page, second by difficulty, third twice the first. Wants to read the tied sheet for pride; shuts her mouth.
- **445 — A Fine Hand In A Room.** Days 1,192–1,195, the 9th to the 12th. THE MIDPOINT. Four round a sheet not hers — young reader, woman with thumb, man with brush, woman of twenty-two eight months gone with a bad night in nine months — asking in the ordinary voice whether she will read it. **The young reader argues against asking her and is right: in front of a room she becomes a hand in that room, and somebody will write it down in a year and call it her choice. The woman of twenty-two asks whether the hand is a bad one, is refused, and does not ask twice.** She reads upper plain and lower fine with a name at the foot; the name goes on the roll, four hundred and six to four hundred and seven. The reversal stands: ch 417's refusal was right, cost everything, taught strangers she can read one; her silence was the evidence, read correctly, bringing the Order to her door; she alone can say she did not expect it. Nothing undone, ch 417 stands. She leaves having made herself the hand that puts four hundred and seven names into the best book on nine days of road.
- **446 — The Book In His Hand.** Days 1,196–1,199, the 13th to the 16th. The roll up close: plain boards, four hundred and seven names with dates, folders shelved with dates on strings, three marks for one reading. A name on it cannot be un-recruited; no hand takes one off; the keeper looked twice for his own. **He touches one reader's shoulder on the way past and goes on, which is the whole of what he gives her and costs him nothing.** Her reading sits tied, dated, entered by her mouth.
- **447 — Two Coppers A Page.** Days 1,200–1,202, the 17th to the 19th. The rate board a third time; claims four for the hard hand; four coppers counted out; her hand stops as over four on a bench and eight on Wei Ran's book. She cannot be paid and never could. Rate stands, book stands. Bread gone by morning; grief for fair arithmetic she cannot touch.
- **448 — Two Yeses Without Meaning.** Days 1,203–1,204, the 20th to the 21st. Finds them on a stone: all right to putting it down as read by the room (thirteenth); all right to staying for the book (seventeenth). Meant as I hear you, taken as yes. Nothing written with her name, both true anyway. **A third pebble, for herself, is set down in the morning and swept off again the same minute; two stay.** Asks to take them back; told there is nothing to take back. **The young reader puts the folder down at the counter edge as he passes and neither of them says a word about a fine hand and a room.**
- **449 — Nothing At All In Daylight.** Day 1,205, the twenty-second. Daylight, road between gate and building. Xie Changyin states the offer in full once more, keeping the count of the one time in ten she is wrong. Ning Yan says nothing for fifteen breaths and through it. Xie Changyin accepts nothing and walks back; then the gate, then the last lamp, then the dark. Nine coppers, no bread, a reading on a roll she cannot take out, two yeses, and a silence that is the only term in the whole of it not yet spent.

## Volume 8 Batch 0003 — *Recruited*, ch 450–464, day 1,206–1,262 — WRITTEN

**Fifteen chapters, 27,879 words of body prose, average ~1,859, zero brackets. Pressure: what he did with her while she was somewhere else.** *Figures re-measured after the Volume 8 Batch 0003 review pass of 2026-10-14, which recast thirty-seven sentences across fourteen of the fifteen chapters without moving a beat; the drafting phase had 27,826 / ~1,855. See `state/continuity.md` §*Established facts, Volume 8 Batch 0003 (ch 450–464)*, §*Construction budget — Volume 8 Batch 0003* and §*Volume 8 Batch 0003 — external review, and the repair pass that answered it*.

- **450 — A Man Off His Feet.** Days 1,206–1,209, the 23rd to the 26th. The silence holds four days and the thing that frightens her is not a term settling but a room going back to work. Bread west: eleven miles in two days, two loaves at the store five miles past the mill, four coppers, nine down to five, the roof cord still holding. Back east and **past the building for the first time**, down a lane she has watched carts use for eleven weeks and never walked: a cut, an oak frame four feet off the bank, a dressed sill, a chain, nine men, and a man of forty on the boards with his leg wrapped. She is told she is not on the list, sits on the low wall for three hours, and is left alone on a floor for the first time in eleven weeks.
- **451 — The Board She Could Read.** Days 1,210–1,213, the 27th to the 30th. The day's sheet into her left hand and not one word of it legible, as in twenty years. She reads the figures instead: six in, eight out, two of them one cart twice, one line with no other half. At the foot, in plain words, a floor writes the shape of a day — loads by the load, a sill by the length, a chain that wants oil, a man's leg — **and she can read every word of his day.** And she finds the ruled column on the left that is empty on every line of the sheet and has been under a pen for a hundred years. At dusk she walks east to the crossing for the first time, stands four feet off a painted board she cannot read, and turns back.
- **452 — What A Floor Writes.** Days 1,214–1,217, the 1st to the 4th. **Mouth four of six**, in a woman of fifty who runs the floor: nine men off her frame in four years; **eleven lines with a day in the left column that she did not write and nobody in her building has**; Zheng's leg on the eleventh day; a frame of four hundred years of oak from which they all come down the wrong way. *I did not think of the two columns. A floor writes the day it finds out. You do not take out a rule because you have not found the thing it is for.* There is a sheet west of here with a heading over the second column and it is not in anybody's hand in that building. A man of forty brings her the sheet she squared, says say the other thing, and she reads it out loud on a bank in the open. She reads the back books and finds eleven dates in the left column, ten of them earlier than the day the floor found out, and **she leaves every one of them exactly as she found it.**
- **453 — Forty Yards Of Sill.** Days 1,218–1,221, the 5th to the 8th. **The number before the act.** At the fifth hour on the fifth, in the dark: *five days and two hours, out of the ground under you, not coming back; it is not for you, it is not for me, I am putting it where somebody's hand already is.* She asks whose hand and is refused once and does not ask twice. He says *tonight*; it goes in the morning. She stands at the crossing on the sixth, seventh and eighth counting lines of chalk she cannot read, and on the eighth a man of fifty comes out of a hut, puts his thumb under a line, reads the board twice, takes a bar off the wall and goes north — without asking where the work was, who wrote it, or whether it will be paid.
- **454 — The Man Who Took The Board.** Days 1,222–1,225, the 9th to the 12th. Forty yards of dressed stone across a made ditch, two days, the last of the light, a lever and four wedges. The near end turns and takes him into the ditch on his back with his leg under the stone's shoulder. **She is forty feet off and can move it the length of its own length and nothing goes — it does not go out of a room she has not been asked into, and nobody asked.** She runs the last forty feet, gets him out in four seconds, and holds the wedges for two hours in the dark while he sets it a foot at a time. Four sentences of talk and no more. He finishes at last light and **walks past the low door in the far bank without turning in at it.** Four wedges is not remarkable anywhere; she finds that out standing in a lane.
- **455 — Four Days Apart.** Days 1,226–1,229, the 13th to the 16th. The man at the crossing comes down the lane four days after the work and tells a woman of fifty about it and she writes the thirteenth in the right-hand column. **The left-hand cell, empty on every line of that sheet for as long as she has known the room, has a date in it: the ninth.** She reads two columns against each other on her knees four inches off the paper and the two dates are four days apart, the earlier one four days before anybody in that building knew there was a sill. She cannot read a word of the hand and cannot tell it from a man who forgot to come down a lane for four days. **She does not write on the line, and she does not say the thing out loud to the woman of fifty, because she does not ask anybody to do anything** — the same rule that stopped a heading being written over a second column nine days of road west.
- **456 — She Says His Name Once.** Days 1,230–1,233, the 17th to the 20th. She sorts the left column across six books: thirteen lines, twelve of them ordinary work nobody on that floor could have known about on the day it was done. **A column gets ruled for a thing somebody expects to have to write in it and stays empty, and once in a while a day comes the column was not made for.** That explains the column. It does not explain the fourth day. And then the thing she has never once had in twenty years of reading columns: **a document with her standing on it.** On the twentieth she says his name once into an empty shed and **something comes back in a quarter of a second, the first time in as long as she can remember. *I know.*** She asks what he knows and he does not tell her. He gives her one thing she did not ask for: *you have got to the column, that is further than anybody has got*, and she works out that being proud of her for it is indistinguishable from a man watching something he started come out right. She says the whole of it out loud to an empty shed, as she has never done about anything, and gets nothing. What she wants is one word off a painted board.
- **457 — One Payment In The Ordinary Hand.** Days 1,234–1,238, the 21st to the 25th. **She asks a man at a gate to read a board to her because she cannot read one**, and he reads it in five pieces with his thumb over the fifth line. The sill is on it again for the twenty-first and he goes north for four hours. **The sill is paid once because he has been down the lane once.** His mouth, in five pieces: nineteen years at the gate, eleven at the pits, nine on a roof, four trades and two of them bad; a wife who does not come to the gate and that is her choice; a yard of sill is a yard of sill, the same rate since he was forty-one; he has done it twice in twelve days and been paid for once, **nobody has come to take the other one back, he is not going to ask for it, and if it does not come he will do it twice again, because that is what the board says.** The woman of fifty refuses her the rate and says why and is right. Nine days of road west is what two years of reading comes to.
- **458 — Nobody Named On The List.** Days 1,239–1,242, the 26th to the 29th. **The fourth frame scene.** Away from her hearing, in the layer's own reading-space, present tense throughout, no other character in a marked present frame. Thirty-one days of listings and not one name on any of them. **He goes looking for one thing: does the layer honour a listing for a person who is not the Chosen One. It will not** — one counterparty, no second door — **and he worked that out on the sixth and it does not matter, because the layer has never paid anybody: what pays is a woman of fifty in a nine-foot room out of what the floor takes in, because a man came down a lane and said he had done a thing.** He finds out he did not put the dates in — a listing carries work, work carries the day it happened and the day anybody found out, and the layer does not distinguish — and he finds out **there is no listing in the space with him on it. He can put work where any hand will find it and he cannot point the thing at himself.** A fourth in his hand since the twenty-second, unspent, and the reason he cannot give her is that she read a column four inches off the paper.
- **459 — The Chain Across An Empty Road.** Days 1,243–1,246, the 30th, the 1st to the 3rd. The chair is empty and the bar is not against the wall. **A boy of fifteen lifts the chain through a cart at the eighth hour and does not go and find out where the man was.** She goes over the nine things she could have done, and the one she cannot put down is that she could have gone four hundred yards up a cart track and looked, and nobody within two days of that gate has ever been asked to look for a man by a stranger who cannot read a board. She carries bundles; nobody thanks her. The woman of fifty says she will go out at the fourth hour on the third, **because she has nine men on a frame and four carts before the middle of the day and a man who has never once not been in that chair.** She does not come back by the shut or by the ninth hour. At the ninth hour Ning Yan walks down anyway. There is a light in the hut.
- **460 — He Was Found At The Crossing.** Days 1,247–1,250, the 4th to the 7th. **The first person this book kills outside a Volume 1 furnace.** A woman in a grey shawl in the back room. He is on the bed on his back, boots off and set square, coat on, hands crossed, leg wrapped, nothing on him and nothing out of place. **Nothing had been done to that man by anybody.** The sill came on his board on the sixth, he read it that evening, he went north on the eighth, he was out two days and he was in the ditch on the second. **A man can carry a thing once. A man cannot carry the same thing twice inside a dozen days, because there is a thing that gets used up, and it is not his arm and it is not his leg, and there is no name for it on this ground.** **Ning Yan says four words in the doorway: it was me.** The woman of fifty answers: then you should have asked him not to go. **And she cannot — she is not on that floor, not in that lane, and no one has ever drawn up a way for a woman who has never met a man to ask him not to carry a sill. She said no out loud. Nobody believed her and nobody disbelieved her.** The book gets one line: he has not come down the lane since the thirteenth.
- **461 — Twice For One Thing.** Days 1,251–1,255, the 8th to the 12th. **Mouth five of six.** Thirteen lines in the left column, twelve of them four years old and one from the fifteenth: a load off the low end of the frame, two of them by the weight, a day with two helpers. **Two figures against that line, four days apart, and the second went into the book on the third of a month and she found it on the eighth.** She finds him on a low wall — Zheng, thirty-four, a leg wrapped — and gets a straight answer: paid once, the sheet has two figures, and **he has been in that room every ninth hour for eleven years and has never once looked at it, because a man does not go and read the book to see what it says about his own work.** Then his mouth, in five pieces: eleven years on a frame and eight of them here, I have not asked why, a wife who does the numbers in a book of her own and does not read this one because this one is not ours to read and has never asked to, a trade, a rate, and he is not going to be asked about a book by a woman he met on a wall. **She believes him, because she has done the same thing in front of a forty-year-old book and a forty-two-year-old book and a shelf of folders and has never once gone to read a book to see what it says about her.**
- **462 — She Makes Him Stop.** Days 1,256–1,258, the 13th to the 15th. **THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE STEP.** The door shut, because a thing said in the open goes out into the air and she has had two years of her own answers going up a road. **Stop.** Once, in one piece: thirteen lines in a left column nobody on that floor fills; Zheng on the fifteenth; two figures four days apart; a man at the gate who laid a sill on the ninth and the same forty yards on the twenty-first and was found on the fourth with his boots off and nothing done to him by anybody; **you told me one number and you told me before you did it and it is the only reason I have any of this at all; you have spent three times and you told me once.** He gives her the one true thing and there is nothing in it to argue with: a listing is work, there is no line in the ground with him on it, and he has never once been able to do the version where it is me. He will not promise. **Then he tells her why he told her once: because she would have asked him not to do it. He takes the fourth one and he puts it down.** She feels it go and it cost her nothing and she did not enjoy it. **She does not thank him, does not forgive him, keeps the two apart on purpose, and does not ask what it was worth.**
- **463 — A Man With Nothing Left.** Days 1,259–1,260, the 16th to the 17th. The first day after is the worst and being right about it does not help. **He says nothing, because he has nothing to spend, and a spend is the only thing he has ever had that is his.** She wanted him angry and what she got was that he gave her the truth and then stopped. Zheng on the frame thirty times up and down, and **she does not tell him, because there is no version of *a voice put forty yards of sill on a board* that a man at a frame can do anything with except be frightened by, and being frightened by it would cost him sleep he has eight days of the leg left for.** The woman in the grey shawl puts a cloth over the chair. **The board is the gate man's office and it will be six before the month turns.** The bar's strap is rebound with four inches off her own coat lining. She says his name once into a shed, nothing comes back, and she had expected it.
- **464 — Eighty-Four Days.** Days 1,261–1,262, the 18th to the 19th. **She counts eighty-four marks into the edge of a board with the point out of the bar, eight rows of ten and four over. She does not count toward anything — eighty-four is what you get when there is nothing on the other end of the count.** In coin, stopping costs nothing: five coppers, no bread since the twenty-eighth of the month before last, a strap she bound herself. In days it costs everything: the furnace will not light itself, every stage has cost somebody a pair of hands in a room for hours, and **nobody is going to turn up at the end of a lane with two hours of a day in them for eighty-four days.** Not hungry is not a gift — a body that does not want anything is a body that had something taken out of it a long time ago. **And the sentence under it all: for eighty-four days nobody pays for anything, and it is the first time in eight years that the reason she cannot get stronger is that she has told somebody to stop.** The woman of fifty comes to the shed, says she has never once seen anybody count, and **tells her that a girl came down the lane twenty days before the thirteenth and stood at that table for four days and said nothing to anybody, and that she has been not saying anything about that for ten days as well, and that is me being exactly the same kind of coward I was about the gate.** One more mark, at the other end of the board, kept for herself. A door that does not shut properly, and she does not fix it.

## Volume 8 Batch 0004 — *Recruited*, ch 465–478, day 1,263–1,340 — VOLUME 8 CLOSED

**Fourteen chapters, 29,125 words of body prose, average ~2,080 after the batch review repair, ONE bracketed layer line at ch 470. Pressure: refusing to be a tool.** *Figures measured by one script on the files as they stand after the repair, which moved the batch from 29,186. Nine findings were closed in that repair — three must-fix — and no chapter was restarted; the record is at `state/continuity.md` §*Volume 8 Batch 0004 — external review, and the repair pass that answered it*. See `state/continuity.md` §*Established facts, Volume 8 Batch 0004 (ch 465–478)*, §*Construction budget — Volume 8 Batch 0004* and §*Self-review pass — Volume 8 Batch 0004*.*

- **465 — The Lane Asking For Nothing.** Days 1,263–1,268, the 20th to the 25th. **Four sheets a day on a bench at the shed with a stone on the top one, and the floor asking her about carts for the first time** — she can add two columns and take one off and a woman of fifty has had nobody who can do it for nineteen years, and the sixth cart came in after the shut. **The woman of fifty's eleven days of silence break: she asks *did you put the dates in my column*, Ning Yan says no, the woman asks *then who does*, and Ning Yan gives her a silence** — her third true answer that is not an answer in four days — and the woman reads it and says *I am not going to ask you again, and that is a different thing from not asking now.* Nobody thanks her for the half; she works out that a sheet a day is a hand on a wheel. **On the twenty-fifth at the ninth hour she asks him for the whole of it, once, and nothing comes back for a length of night**, and she understands for the first time that a man with nothing to spend is not a man who has chosen not to spend one. **She cannot ask him twice — and that is what ch 466 comes out of.**
- **466 — One Thousand One Hundred And Nine.** Days 1,269–1,272, the 26th to the 29th. **THE BILL.** At the fourth hour on the twenty-sixth, in the ordinary voice, in the present tense, once: **one thousand one hundred and nine days and six hours, the whole of it, from the eighth of the month it started in to the twenty-sixth of this one** — and **twenty-one days and four hours, which is what he put where a hand would find it, three times on this ground, of which he told her one.** Then one sentence: **you did not ask me.** She makes him say it again and it comes out identically, which tells her it was never worked out the night before. **She writes it on a board and scrapes it off, because a column is a request.** The last observation of the chapter is the one that carries the volume: **he has never once called it a kindness, and a thing that is never named is not a decision anybody made.**
- **467 — Seventeen Hours Out Of Every Hundred.** Days 1,273–1,276, the 30th and the 1st to the 3rd. Four days of arithmetic on the boards. **Twenty-six thousand six hundred and twenty-two hours; about six thousand five hundred days in an eighteen-year life; seventeen out of every hundred; a little over three years and eleven days out of the four years she has been a person a place can put its hand on — and not one day of it she can point at, because it did not come out of a day.** **One in fifty-three is everything she caught in seven weeks of counting**, said out loud under the woman of fifty's ears and refused by her. Then the hole — one thousand and eighty-eight days and two hours — and the finding of the four days: **it is not for the work. The twenty-one days are the times he did a thing. The rest is the price of being findable, once, from outside.** She does not find out who set the price, will not go through the door in it, and puts it nowhere. **She ends wanting a second column: she has the two dates of the pair and there is no book on nine days of road with a column for it.**
- **468 — The Board Behind The Counter.** Days 1,277–1,280, the 4th to the 7th. **Seventy-five days since she held a silence in daylight, and nothing has stopped, and nobody has asked her one single question** — which is not a silence she keeps. The rate board a fourth time; her own empty hand flat on the wood for two seconds. **Wei Ran at the gate — her second and last Volume 8 card — four journeys east and two back, none of the four with anything in it, said as a trade; eight coppers still on the book and never chased; and then she says *Ning Yan* out loud in a road with four men unloading within nine feet.** That is the whole mechanism of the ground: one person says a name in a road and every one of them hears it again. **The young reader of twenty-six pays his thread in four sentences and no scene** — *I am not going to ask you to read anything. I said a thing in that room about you and the room did it anyway, and I have had four months to work out what it cost me to be right, and the answer is nothing.* **Xie Changyin has not been told anything and does not ask**: the offer is final after the twelfth and a woman of thirty-one will make it, and she is going up the road for the season.
- **469 — A Sheet Under A Lamp.** Days 1,281–1,285, the 8th to the 12th. A fine hand, tied, dated on a slip of paper, four days on a bench, four people failing at it. **She says nothing for four days and does not say why.** When she is asked in a room she says no, and then tells the room the thing she has never told anybody: **you cannot read it because there is one of me, and I have been able to read it for eight years, and I did not learn it anywhere and was never taught it — and the reason I know what a fine hand looks like is that I have spent two years refusing to have one read to me, and you cannot refuse a thing you have not recognised.** What she does not say is that there is a sheet about *her*, and that her refusal is why a woman of sixty-one walked eleven miles. Xie Changyin promises, in the ordinary voice, that the room will not ask again. It goes east on the twenty-second on a string. **Her want at the close: she wanted to read it more than anything in nine months, and there is no instrument on nine days of road for reading one without being entered by it.**
- **470 — The Ceiling Of A Realm.** Days 1,286–1,292, the 13th to the 19th. **THE BATCH MIDPOINT, AND THE ONLY BRACKET IN THE BATCH.** Four days of the ring coming up a finger's width and stopping, and **on the fourth she stops doing it on purpose**, because for eighty-four days nobody is paying for anything and she will not beg for a second pair of hands. **Xie Changyin dates the end of the offer: the twelfth, and then the road for the season.** On the eighteenth at the fourth hour, in the long room with the shutters up, **the ninth stage arrives without her asking and cannot be stopped** — she says no out loud and the no does not take, **which is ch 434's cost paid in the worst place it can**. [Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage nine, is held.] Then nothing, which is the part that frightens her — two years of silence from the layer and nothing behind her at all. She gets out into the road and lies in the dirt and the gate man does not come over. **Cost, named once: the small open place low behind her fourth rib comes open again and does not close, and there is nothing above her to close it with.** **There is nothing above her.** The door is a document against her ribs that has never been opened, and **she does not open it, and the reason is not fear — it is that at ch 474 she is going to learn what it is to have a document read aloud to you, and the ninth stage turned up four days before that in daylight with forty people in the room.**
- **471 — The Version Where He Is There.** Days 1,293–1,297, the 20th to the 24th. A fine hand with a girl of twenty-two on it and a month of her life in it. Ning Yan says no twice and **wants to use him**. Then, out loud, in the present tense, with four people in the room, **the Hand is refused** — four people heard a woman of twenty-two refuse a man with a brush and were correct — **and the reason is given for the first time: a hand only works where somebody's hand already is; put it in front of her and the next one goes in front of a man's hand, and a man at a gate takes a bar off a wall because a painted board said so; and he has never once been able to do the version where he is there to see what it does.** She says out loud, in one sentence, that this is **not** ch 419's reason: that one was about her, this one is about the shape of it, and **it does not protect anybody**. **It costs her nothing** — the first refusal in seven years that comes out clean — **and she finds that worse than being taken from.** The cart goes east on the twenty-eight with the girl's whole life on it.
- **472 — What A Form Is.** Days 1,298–1,301, the 25th to the 28th. Worked out on a cart sheet at the end of the lane. **A form is a page with a place on it for a person to put themselves** — and the hinge of the whole trade: **the sheet is read to you because you cannot read it yourself**, because the paper is in the fine hand, a paper can be folded into four and taken home or read out of a room by somebody paid two coppers a page, **and it is read to you so that there is no copy, not because it is fair.** Recruitment, said by her and not by anybody else: **a reading performed on a person, by somebody qualified to read it, in a room, on the condition that the person confirms or denies** — the same act as the sheet at ch 417 with her name at the head of it. **The maximum: one rate, four coppers for every page, no second and no third, and the board behind the counter is wrong and she will not have it repainted.** Xie Changyin dates the reading for the second at the fourth hour with the man who keeps the book in the room.
- **473 — The Sheet Read Aloud To Her.** Days 1,302–1,305, the 29th, the 30th and the 1st to the 2nd. The first of a month, at the fourth hour, with the shutters up. Five terms, all fair. The sixth line, the shortest on the paper: **this document has been read to you, do you agree to it.** **She asks for the column of what it costs, which is the one thing she wanted from this trade**, and gets it in Xie Changyin's own hand, unvouched and not read aloud, because **there is no hand in the world that can check a figure a woman has put down in her own hand** — which is exactly what happened to Ning Yan at a mill when she was fifteen or sixteen. Four lines: four fingers, half an arm, the turn, the order — **and a fifth line that is not a cost: nine of them are in that room and I do not know one of their names.** She agrees to answer on the second. **Xie Changyin tells her, unasked, that her heart is going at about nine in the morning.**
- **474 — Her Name In The Best Book.** Days 1,306–1,309, the 3rd to the 6th. **THE VOLUME'S AND THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE STEP.** She reads the sheet **to them first**, in four seconds, and tells the room the reason she can read a hand they cannot — because her refusal in a room four months back is why a trade of eleven hundred years is sending a sheet it cannot read east on a cart — and then says **no**, with no reason in front of it. **Xie Changyin writes it and reads it back to her in the ordinary voice before it goes in**, which is the ceremony turned the right way round once in its history. **Ning Yan makes her take back the one line that is hers** and the woman refuses: a thing not put in is not a refusal, it is a person who did not turn up. **So she reads her own refusal back to them**, and a man of sixty-one writes one line and the date in the ordinary hand. **It went in. There is no hand in that building that takes it out.** It is the fourth entry of her life and the first one she chose.
- **475 — What A Quiet Ground Looks Like.** Days 1,310–1,314, the 7th to the 11th. Four days, in order, and **it does not happen on one day**. **The bench is empty on the seventh** and the woman of fifty says the reason: **there is nothing coming down the lane for me to check** — a cart brings a sheet, a cart brings a thing, and what is on the carts is what the building has read. **She bought her own anonymity with her own usefulness**, said out loud on the bank and again under a hedge. **The man of sixty-one: the folder goes east on the first with nothing on it, and there is nowhere in the book to put that, and he has been looking since the fifth of a month.** The counter asks whether there is something and she says there is not. **The young reader of twenty-six puts it in one word — *two* — and then says what nobody else will: that what has stopped is not the reading, it is a woman of fifty putting a number on a bench for six days and then not doing it on the seventh.** **Under the hedge she admits the want she has never admitted — she wants to be the sort of person a ground reaches for, without it going into a book, and no arrangement on nine days of road has ever offered it.**
- **476 — The Price Of Not Being Found.** Days 1,315–1,319, the 12th to the 16th. No one to ask; the Hunter's two pages are spent and she is not going to go and find him. Five things have ever come for her in eight years and **all five came out of a document** — there is no road into her life except paper, and that is eight years old and she is the only person alive who could have found it. **A person who is not read is harder to find**, worked out four feet off a post at a gate: the hand on a gate reads a painted board because the board is where its hands are, and take the paper away and the hand does not stop. **The third finding is the one she is not ready for: she is at the top of a realm at eighteen with no wages, no season, no pay she can take and no institution that has heard of her — and it is the best thing that has happened to her in two years, and she chose it, and she cannot say so out loud to anybody including him.** She writes the whole list on a board and one line at the bottom, well away from the rest.
- **477 — A Description, And It Is Accurate.** Days 1,320–1,324, the 17th to the 21st. **MOUTH SIX OF SIX.** Xie Changyin walks two miles down the lane at the sixth hour, sixty-one, carrying nothing, and puts the whole of it in pieces and not once as a speech: *you take a sheet four people cannot take and make it into numbers, and you say them out loud to whoever is in the room, and you do it for nothing, and you have been paid for it once in four coppers and your hand stopped. That is the trade.* **The trap named by the woman selling it: what she has spent five months trying to hire her for is what she has been doing for nothing since she was sixteen, and the only thing there is to sell anybody is that somebody will be standing in the room when she does it.** **The one wrong sentence, said once and refused once and not argued: *a person on a roll is safer than a person off it.*** **The one thing she wins: the room will not ask her again, the tied sheet goes east unopened, and a girl of twenty-two goes on not knowing what a hand said about her** — and Xie Changyin says she will not thank her and will not apologise, because both would be deciding how Ning Yan feels about a thing done on purpose. Then she gives the shelf, the room and the date on the string for the two sheets a woman wrote nine years ago and did not sign, **and calls it an ordinary consequence and not a favour.** Ning Yan writes the address on a board, scrapes it off, and writes it again anyway.
- **478 — Forty-One Lines On A Rail.** Days 1,325–1,340, the 22nd to the 7th, and **VOLUME 8'S LAST PAGE.** **Thirteen miles west in three days, the whole distance on the page.** **One loaf at the store five miles west of the mill, two coppers, five down to three, because she wanted it**, and it was the best thing she has put in her mouth in four years. The roof cord holds; the ring is fed; the door does not shut properly and she does not fix it. **She does not say his name for eleven days, which has never happened — eleven occasions in seven years, four of them answered, every one of them inside a dozen days of a spend.** At the sixth hour on the seventh she reads all forty-one lines on the rail down twice, gets them the same twice, **and can take none of them.** Then **she notices she has been standing there eleven minutes and nothing has happened** — not the boy, not the man behind the counter, not the man with the brush. A month ago four people on that floor asked her a question a month and wrote the answer down; a woman of fifty asked her about carts every day for two months. **Nothing on nine days of road wants anything from her at all.** Three coppers, bread for a day, eighteen years old, at the top of a realm, nothing above her, and a piece of paper against her ribs she has not opened. **She says his name once into a shed with boards on the floor, and it is the twelfth time in seven years and the first one that has not come inside a dozen days of something happening to him. He has had nothing to spend for eighty-four days and there is nothing left to answer with. And she waits anyway.**

---

## Volume 8 closed — audited

**Volume 8, *Recruited*, ch 421–478, fifty-eight chapters, day 1,091 to day 1,340, 250 days. Closed and audited by the
Volume 8 close phase, which opened no chapter, wrote no prose and changed no beat. Full record at
`workspace/volume-08-close/SUMMARY.md` and `state/continuity.md` §*Volume 8 close — repairs* and §*Volume 8 close — the
volume as a whole*. **The close was then reviewed and four of its own figures were repaired without prose being written;
no chapter, beat, day, title or summary below changed. Record at `state/current.md` §*Volume 8 close review — REPAIR*.**

**105,846 words of body prose over fifty-eight chapters, average ~1,825**, on the method the close settles for Volume 8 —
**every line not beginning `#`**, so the `#` chapter title line and every `##` section heading are excluded. On the other
convention, `##` headings counted as prose, the same files are **107,669, average ~1,856**. **Both sets travel with every
figure. 478 `#` headings manuscript-wide, zero collisions, longest 39 characters.** **Two bracketed layer lines in
fifty-eight, at ch 434 and ch 470; the budget is spent and Volume 9 opens on no bracket.**

### The volume-level entry

**In this volume, Ning Yan must refuse the Order, or she becomes exactly the tool Yuan hated — fixed by
`outline/series.md` §9, answered on the page, and it is answered in a room at the fourth hour of the third of a month with
the shutters up.** She is the strongest person in every room she walks into and it turns out to be a qualification. The
offer is made in daylight, on the ordinary condition, by a woman of sixty-one who is right about nine things in ten, and
every term of it is reasonable and one of them is fatal, because it makes her a post. **She reads the document to the
room before she refuses it, because nobody in that room can read it and she is the only person on nine days of road who
can.** She says no with no reason in front of it. Xie Changyin writes one line and reads it back, Ning Yan makes her take
back the one line that is hers, the woman refuses on the ground that a thing not put in is not a refusal, and **Ning Yan
reads her own refusal back to her.** A man of sixty-one writes one line and the date in the ordinary hand. **It went in.
There is no hand in that building or on that ground that takes it out.** And the price is not persecution: **the Order
stops reading her**, and at the last page **nothing on nine days of road wants anything from her at all**, which is the
best thing that has happened to her in two years and which she chose and cannot say so out loud to anybody including him.

### One line per batch

- **Batch 0001 (ch 421–434, day 1,091–1,152) — AN ARRIVAL. 27,666 words, average ~1,976. One bracket at ch 434.**
  A strip comes back down the mill road on a cart that does not go east, dated the eighteenth with nothing on it but a
  date. **Xie Changyin, sixty-one, walks eleven miles into a shed at a mill at ch 423.** The two lines and the date are
  quoted back to him out loud by a stranger, the offer is stated in full inside eight chapters, a vouched floor sheet
  goes out at ch 427, **she says a date out loud to a stranger at ch 432 and cannot take it back at ch 434**, and
  **Nascent Soul 8 arrives on the pinned day with the cost named once: she cannot call a halt — herself, once a thing
  she is in has started.**
- **Batch 0002 (ch 435–449, day 1,153–1,205) — BEING WANTED. 21,681 words, average ~1,445. No brackets.**
  **Eleven miles and three days on foot on a body with one arm at half, a turnstile she is turned back at once, and a
  low lime-washed building with more lamps in it than anything she has stood in.** Six structural-answer chapters, four
  hundred and seven names, one rate, and **at ch 445 she reads a fine hand in a room because four people there cannot,
  and it goes on a roll** — which is the batch's irreversible step, and the volume's midpoint, and the bill for the best
  decision she made in two volumes. **This is the volume's thin passage: thirteen of the volume's nineteen chapters under
  the 1,800 guide are in these fifteen, and it is where the volume's own argument happens.**
- **Batch 0003 (ch 450–464, day 1,206–1,262) — WHAT HE DID WITH HER WHILE SHE WAS SOMEWHERE ELSE. 27,879 words,
  average ~1,859. No brackets.** He surfaces a listing to a man at a gate who is not her; she reads a second column on
  somebody else's floor sheet at ch 455 and finds two dates four days apart on one line; **the fourth frame scene is on
  the page at ch 458**, away from her hearing; **a man east of the turn does not come back at ch 460**, four days before
  she works out why; **and at ch 462 she makes him stop, out loud, on the boards, in a shed with the door shut, and he
  puts a listing down and never picks it up again.**
- **Batch 0004 (ch 465–478, day 1,263–1,340) — REFUSING TO BE A TOOL. 28,620 words, average ~2,044. One bracket at
  ch 470.** **The bill at ch 466, in a number, unasked for a second time: one thousand one hundred and nine days and six
  hours**, of which twenty-one days and four hours is the work he put where a hand would find it, and **she writes it on
  a board and scrapes it off because a column is a request.** **Nascent Soul 9 at ch 470 on the pinned day, arriving
  without her asking and without her being able to stop it, and the door is a document she has not opened.** **The Hand
  refused once out loud in a room at ch 471, with the reason given for the first time, and it costs her nothing.** The
  offer in full at the maximum. **THE VOLUME'S IRREVERSIBLE STEP AT CH 474 — she refuses the Order on the record,
  read back to her before it goes in, and there is no hand that takes it out.** The ground goes quiet at ch 475–477.
  **And at ch 478 nothing on nine days of road wants anything from her at all.**

### One line per structural item

- **Nascent Soul 8, ch 434, day 1,152, the twenty-ninth of the month beginning 1,124. Cost: she cannot call a halt.**
- **Nascent Soul 9, ch 470, day 1,291, the eighteenth of the month beginning 1,274. Cost: there is nothing above her and
  the door is a document.** Intervals of **76** and **139** days; **139 is the longest single interval in the manuscript.**
- **Two bracketed layer lines**, ch 434 and ch 470, far-side house form, **and no others.**
- **The bill, ch 466.** One thousand one hundred and nine days and six hours.
- **The frame scene, ch 458.** The fourth in the manuscript and the only marked present frame in Volume 8.
- **The refusal, ch 474.** The fourth entry of her life and the first one she chose.
- **The six structural-answer mouths**, ch 424 · 437 · 443 · 452 · 461 · 477.
- **The Hunter's two pages**, ch 428 and ch 440, and his name on the page once, at ch 440.
- **Wei Ran's two cards**, ch 441 and ch 468.
- **The four spendings and the four refusals to spend**, Batch 0003's three spends and ch 462's stop, Batch 0004's
  refusal at ch 471 and eighty-four days of nothing.
- **The 250 days**, 1,091 to 1,340, and **1,340 − 1,256 = 84**, the length of his silence, which ch 478 says out loud.

### The volume's closing image

**She says his name once into a shed with boards on the floor, and nothing comes back, and she waits anyway.** That is
the last day of this month and she is not going to count it and she is not going to put a mark on a board. **She lay on
the boards at the end of the seventh day of a month with three coppers in a fold and a bar against a wall and a door that
does not shut properly, waiting for a man who has nothing to say, and she was not going to be anywhere else.** The wind
came in under the door at about a foot a second. The boards were cold. **There was nothing above her in the world and
there was one door in it and she had not opened it.** She waited anyway.

**That is the last page of Volume 8 and the first thing Volume 9 opens on.**

| 482 | 1,355–1,359 | **The Store Five Miles West** | she asks one question too many about bread nobody buys and is told so, kindly |
| 483 | 1,360–1,364 | **What The Bread Costs** | she does not fix the door that does not shut, on purpose, **because fixing it would be keeping** |
| 484 | 1,365–1,369 | **The Woman Who Never Asked** | **structural mouth one of five** — the store woman who has sold bread for two years and never asked her name |
| 485 | 1,370–1,374 | **Eleven Minutes At The Rail** | she stands eleven minutes and finds that nothing on nine days of road wants anything from her, and leaves before a twelfth |
| 486 | 1,375–1,379 | **Counting Toward Nothing** | **the midpoint.** She writes eighty-five forward for thirty-nine days after it stopped being true, catches herself doing it, and stops counting toward anything |
| 487 | 1,380–1,384 | **The Door That Does Not Shut** | she decides to open the paper alone and tells nobody, including him |
| 488 | 1,385–1,389 | **The Paper Opened Alone** | **the irreversible step, and the batch climax.** She opens it at the fifth hour of the twenty-second of a month with a bar under the door and four fingers and a thumb. **It cannot be un-opened** |
| 489 | 1,390–1,394 | **What She Read Inside** | the inside describes a door and leaves the opening to her; she puts it back against her ribs and tells nobody |
| 490 | 1,395–1,399 | **The Count After Opening** | nothing on the ground treats her differently, so she keeps sleeping in the shed to test whether staying still keeps |
| 491 | 1,400–1,404 | **Twelve Feet Off** | **the Hunter, first of his two appearances in Volume 9.** He is nine feet off; she stops twelve. She asks whether a thing written about a person without being read to them can be answered; he answers; neither writes it down |
| 492 | 1,405–1,408 | **The First Step Past** | she stops waiting to be asked and prepares the ring alone |
| 493 | 1,409–1,412 | **Where Staying Stops Counting** | **Severance 1 at the fourth hour of the nineteenth of a month, day 1,412.** Cost named once: **she cannot keep a place by staying in it.** She sleeps in the shed and wakes knowing it is not hers, and does not weep, and leaves at the seventh hour |

### What the batch established, for whoever drafts Batch 0002

- **Severance 1 is on the page.** Day 1,412, the nineteenth of the month beginning 1,394, **121 days after Nascent Soul 9
  at day 1,291 and 270 days before Severance 3 at day 1,561.** She is **past the ceiling by one stage.**
- **The paper is open and has never been given a trade term.** It says *Foundation the first stage* on the front of it
  and it is still folded in four against her ribs. **Twenty-seven days passed between opening it on day 1,385 and the
  stage on day 1,412** — not nineteen, which was the figure in the first issue of the ch 489 card and in
  `outline/volume-09.md`'s power-progression table, and which was derived from nothing.
- **He is not on the page in this batch at all.** Zero occurrences of his name across the fifteen chapters, and she
  says his name once, at ch 479, into an empty shed.
- **Severance 1's cost, named once at ch 493 and not to be re-derived:** she cannot keep a place by staying in it. Not
  her arm, not her hand — a shed she has slept in for two years stops being hers for having slept in it.
- **Her position at the last page:** eighteen years old, four fingers and a thumb, right arm open half the time, eight
  of eleven holds, short breath at four counts everywhere, no money, no wages, no bread, no season, a bar she cannot
  hold at the heavy end, and four miles of lane east of a mill whose wheel has not turned since before she came.

### Deviations from the drafting prompt, recorded rather than hidden

1. **ch 491's title.** The card read *Nine Feet Off*; the page reads **Twelve Feet Off**, and the page is right —
   the Hunter is nine feet off the side of the track and she stops twelve, which is three further than last time. The
   title was moved onto the action rather than onto the other character, and no other title in the batch drifted.

### What the batch established, for whoever drafts Batch 0003

- **Severance 2 is on the page** at day 1,488, seventy-six days after Severance 1, and **Severance 3 is 321 days away.**

- **He has not been on the page in fifteen chapters. The run from day 1,256 stands at 232 days and breaks at ch 520.**
- **Her position at the last page:** eighteen years old, four fingers and a thumb, right arm open four days in five, eight
  of eleven holds, short breath at four counts everywhere including inside a loaded reach, no money, no wages, no bread
  since the second of a month, no shed, and a dish with clay four inches up off flat ground.

### Deviations from the plan, recorded rather than hidden

1. **Ch 508's shed is the shed at the end of the lane and not a shed four miles east of a mill.** The scene the outline
   pins is kept whole — he shuts a door, he waits for the latch, she is not in it — and the turnstile is left uncrossed so
   that the one crossing of it can happen at ch 518. Full reasoning at `state/continuity.md` §*Three readings taken
   against the outline*.
2. **The misreading's two months run past the end of this batch**, from ch 498 at day 1,440 to about ch 510. It is
   established at full local height here and carried live into Batch 0003.
3. **The copy at ch 502 is a page about her, not the kept-or-lost entry.** The rule is *written once*, so the entry is
   Batch 0004's at ch 536 and nothing in Batch 0003 may write it.

### Volume 9 Batch 0002 — review repair, applied to ch 502, ch 505 and ch 507

**Three defects closed and one false inherited note corrected. No beat, day, title, stage, bracket or planned event
moved.** Ch 507's *nineteen days ago somebody wrote down where I sleep* contradicted its own frame — the page was
written on **the third of the month beginning 1,484, in daylight, on a bench at a crossing**, and ch 508 says so in its own
words — and now reads *This morning somebody wrote down where I sleep. I have not slept in it since and I am not going
to*, which also sets up ch 508's four hundred yards. Ch 502's four retrospective markers (*three days since*, *for three
days*, *for four days*, *for four days*) described an interval that its own dates put at **one day** and now read *in the
hours since*, *since the seventh hour of the third*, *since the third* and *since the third*. Ch 505 stated the same beat
twice inside three lines and now states it once, broken at the beat, with its best line intact. **The batch is 28,532
words of body prose on method B after the repair — unchanged, and that is a measurement: ch 505 −13, ch 502 +8, ch 507
+5, a net of zero** — and the average is unchanged at ~1,902, the range unchanged at 1,767 to 2,133, the bracket count
unchanged at **one, at ch 508**, and the forbidden-term count unchanged at **0** across all 21. Full record, plus the staging pinned for Batch 0003 and the two

### One line per chapter

| Ch | Days | Title | What changes on the page |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 509 | 1,489–1,491 | **What The Page Is Worth** | the inventory — no money, no wages, no bread since the third, no shed — **the bar tested on flat ground at ninety feet and the finding that a page can find her only where she was**, and the decision out loud not to go back to the shed at the end of the lane |
| 510 | 1,492–1,494 | **What They Think Is Coming** | **nobody asks her for anything for a whole shift in the room with the six day books**, eleven names said aloud in nine seconds and not one of them looking at her; the anger that is clean and cold; and at the seventh hour a loaf on a bench four miles east that a leg man puts out and does not put out |
| 511 | 1,495–1,497 | **A Line On No Board** | **a stone carried up eleven feet of stair past a painted mark, unasked and unboarded, on a body at half**; the fourth count gone for four seconds at the tenth foot; eleven men in the books and not one stone; **and something coming up under her boots on flat ground a mile from the cut** |
