# VOLUME 12 BATCH 0001 — *the stair*, ch 661–675

**Fifteen chapters, 29,996 words of body prose on method B, average 2,000. Day 2,061 to day 2,084. Pressure: the stair.
Severance 9 throughout, no stage taken, no bracket spent, no frame scene. One irreversible step, at ch 674.
Re-measured by the Batch 0001 review repair; the day range read 2,061 to 2,090 and the figure 29,992 until then.
*Corrected in place by the Volume 12 close review repair, which took four words off ch 670 with the bar's origin date:
29,996 is the live figure and the 30,000 above it is struck.*

- **ch 661, day 2,061, *The Word She Does Not Have*. DECISION.** The eighth of the month beginning 2,054, dawn at the
  turn and then west on foot past the post at nine miles and the mill with the wheel that has not turned in nine years.
  **She tries the sentence out loud at three points on the road and finds that `I am going up` has no object in it** —
  and says that out loud at a gatepost, which is the first use she has ever made of it. She wants a room, not a name or a
  hand. Four miles up, the tower, four flights, the third landing. The boy of sixteen meets her on the top step with two
  buckets and says he has kept the lamp going and does not want to do it on his own. 501 days.
- **ch 662, day 2,062, *Four Flights To A Burning Cage*. DISCOVERY.** She wakes on the copying floor and cannot get up,
  because a keeper's lamp burns while the keeper is in the building and there has been no keeper for seventeen days.
  **The finding is that a boy of sixteen has been carrying oil up four flights twice a day since the twenty-second
  because it went dark at the second hour and he did not want to be in the building in the dark** — and that is the only
  thing that has made her want to be in a room. The stair: four counts, five on the second, seven that will not come down
  on the third landing. The drawer is locked and he has never had the key and has not knocked on it once. He ends on
  the bottom of the stair: he asked the carter on the first where the bundle goes and is not saying it on the fourth floor.
- **ch 663, day 2,063, *Where A Bundle Goes*. REVELATION.** The tenth of the month beginning 2,054, the yard, the boy
  relaying what a carter of sixty-four told him on the ninth after nine days of holding it. **The first time in eleven
  volumes that anybody has said out loud that a bundle goes somewhere: a bundle is not county paper, it is what a thing
  is put in when it is going to be said, through a low barred door on the landing above the floor, four times in
  thirty-one years, the last nine years ago, and the man who came out of the door never let the carter past him.** She
  works out that the bundle's going and the bundle's arriving are one fact and not two. She is angry at a carter who has
  been correct for thirty-one years. He goes past on the cart and says *it goes up* and *not one person has ever come
  down for it*, and does not stop.
- **ch 664, day 2,064, *Four Sheets And A Lock*. EMOTIONAL CONSEQUENCE.** The eleventh of the month beginning 2,054, the
  sixth hour, four sheets of a fair hand on the bench under the window. **She counts them — four sheets, seven sentences,
  one hand — and does not read one word of them, and does not copy them**, and then **puts them back in the locked drawer
  and locks it and puts the key in the front of her bag and tells the boy where they are**, because she is not going to
  carry a dead man's handwriting into a room she has never seen. She finds out what she has actually done — put a thing
  down in a building instead of on her own person — and she hands the boy three instructions at once and **is corrected by
  a boy of sixteen and takes the first one only.** The book: five blank leaves and a nib and an ink she has never let dry.
- **ch 665, day 2,065, *A Question With Two Halves*. IRREVERSIBLE: SHE ASKS.** The twelfth of the month beginning 2,054,
  sixth hour, standing, in daylight, at the east end of the bench, with the boy four feet away at the rack. The terms said
  out loud first: one at a time, in daylight, standing. **Eight years of never asking him about the world, and the reason
  was that every question she wanted was two questions.** She asks it: *Can a person be announced without being
  designated* — three times, because a question said once has not been said — and gets the first half: **an announcement
  goes where there is something to receive it.** The second half is in a room and a person in the room and a hand. **She
  feels the lever come up — asking again would not be asking two things at once, because the first had been answered —
  and refuses it out loud, in the room, so that he will know it is a refusal and not an oversight.**
**This chapter carries one of the two invalid month-begins the Batch 0001 review repair flagged and did not guess —
  *the fourteenth of the month beginning 2,034*, where 2,034 is not a month start. See the flagged list in
  `state/open-threads.md`. *RESOLVED at the Volume 12 close review repair, which set the date deliberately: the page reads
  the fourteenth of the month beginning 2,024 and the fourteenth of the month beginning 1,904, and no instance of 2,034
  survives anywhere in ch 661–720. Full record in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 12 CLOSE — REVIEW REPAIR*.*
- **ch 666, day 2,066, *Four Floors And No Answer*. COST.** The thirteenth of the month beginning 2,054, a whole day in
  a lit room with nothing to do in it. **She cannot take a sheet off the pile: the moment her hand is on a county sheet
  her hand is in a county column, and a girl with a bar has no standing whatever in that building.** Nine sheets a day,
  one boy, nineteen days of two days' work in one. She reads the day's nine announcements and finds six names and forty-four
  days and a rate paid on the seventh, the seventeenth and the twenty-seventh, **and not one of the six has ever had the
  sum read out to him.** The price of not asking twice, said out loud on a landing at the ninth hour: nine days of
  nothing, and no road back for either of them.
- **ch 667, days 2,067–2,068, *A Sentence He Cannot Finish*. REVELATION.** Two days of not asking, and then the first half
  said properly at the sixth hour of the fifteenth: **an announcement is not made to be heard, it is made to arrive; if
  there is a person in the place it arrives and if there is not one it goes into the ground with no sign that it did not;
  it is not the announcement that has to be found, it is the room.** Nine times announced, nine times into the ground,
  every one of them on a road or at a wall. **The second half he cannot finish because it is a set of instructions and it
  is addressed to a body**, and he has never been in a room. She asks him for the smallest possible question instead and
  gets the one thing he has: *they are still there*, because a carter does not carry an empty square of oilcloth up a hill.
- **ch 668, day 2,069, *The Long Table And The Grille*. THE MIDPOINT.** The sixteenth of the month beginning 2,054, the
  third hour, eight flights up a mountain and back down in fifty minutes with two sits. **The room is empty and has been
  for nine years**: a long table down the middle, nothing on it, a lamp on a shelf that is out, a candle stub gone into
  its dish, no chairs. The bar is **on the landing side**, put there from the corridor by somebody who then went down and
  did not come back up. **The finding, in one fact and not two: there has been nothing to receive an announcement in that
  room for nine years and there has not been anybody to receive one for nine years, and neither is a fault and both are
  the same fault.** She does not go through the door. The five blank leaves stay blank.
- **ch 669, day 2,070, *The Instrument In Her Coat*. COST.** The seventeenth of the month beginning 2,054, the bench,
  the eight leaves laid out. **Three things in her hand and none of them is the thing: a stub of a nib, a cake of ink,
  and three fingers and a thumb with the index gone.** The boy offers to write and is better at a county hand than she is
  and she says no and gives the reason twice — first that he is sixteen, then the real one, that she will not spend being
  alone on every one of these on a piece of paper. **The rule is said out loud in daylight with the nib and the ink on the
  bench: this hand cannot write a line that is not true, and a lie is not what that means.** Everything she owns walks.
  510 days.
- **ch 670, days 2,070–2,071, *The Same Four Miles*. DECISION.** Down the mountain, down the long way round the tanner's,
  thirteen miles west. She stops at the mill for the first time in five years and says out loud, on a made road, that a
  room shut for nine years and a wheel that has not turned for nine years are two numbers that happen to be the same, and
  that she is not going to build anything on that. **She works out the arithmetic of the first from the turn: the
  twenty-fourth is six days off and the first of the next month is thirteen.** She tells the woman of sixty, out loud, that
  she is going to stand at the two posts, and why — that everything she has found out in six days has been standing on a
  notice that tells four hundred people where she is. **Both figures corrected by the Batch 0001 review repair: the first
  of the month beginning 2,084 is day 2,084 and the eighteenth is day 2,071, and the difference is thirteen, not
  fourteen.** **This chapter also carries one of the two invalid month-begins the repair flagged and did not guess —
  *the fifth of the month beginning 1,914*, where 1,914 is not a month start and the origin of the bar is dated nowhere
  else. See the flagged list in `state/open-threads.md`. *RESOLVED at the Volume 12 close review repair: the bar carries no
  date at all and the page reads *she has carried it since she was nine*, which is what ch 720 says at the volume's last
  page. Full record in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 12 CLOSE — REVIEW REPAIR*.*
- **ch 671, day 2,072, *The Woman Who Was Right*. EMOTIONAL CONSEQUENCE.** The nineteenth of the month beginning 2,054,
  a whole day on the bench. **She says her own name out loud three times in the grit and works out that on a road a name
  is a thing you say to a person and it means you know them, and you do not know her.** She works out that a board is not
  for the person on it: it is for everybody else. **The woman of sixty tells her the true thing she had not worked out —
  her name went up on the second of a month beginning 1,806 and she was on that board for four of nineteen years, and the
  west road stopped, and a notice about a person who is not doing anything is a notice that gets walked past** — and then
  the thing that costs: **being seen was not a kindness, because a kindness goes both ways and a notice does not, and the
  day the notice stops they stop.** She does not say what happens to a notice after the first and is not going to be asked.
- **ch 672, day 2,073, *The Reason For Two Posts*. DISCOVERY.** The twentieth of the month beginning 2,054, the sixth
  hour, four hundred yards east of the turn, in daylight, with four people going past. She fits her thumb in the groove
  round three sides of the slate and sets the nib and the ink on the lip of the frame and **does not write**. Then she says
  it out loud, in public, and it cannot be said back: **a board is the only thing anybody in this world has ever paid her
  for, and a board is the only thing the fraction comes off, and they are one object and not two.** One in twenty of 513
  is twenty-five and a half days. **The only reason the sum has never come off her is that she has never been on a board,
  and the thing that kept her alive is the thing she has been calling a humiliation.** She also says out loud, once, that
  not being seen is what stopped her finding out whether her brother is alive. She is getting on the board.
  **The 513 is corrected by the Batch 0001 review repair and the page said 510: the series is D − 1,560, verified at ch
  658, 659 and 660, and day 2,073 − 1,560 = 513.**
- **ch 673, days 2,074–2,076, *A Hand And No Reader*. COST.** Three days at the frame and the man with the board on the
  third. She watches a thumb wipe a slate in the time it takes a man to turn round twice. **She says the part about not
  being paid out loud, standing, before anybody has offered it to her and before she has asked one question, so that not
  one person in the county has to be generous to her.** He asks her instead, in order: whether she is not frightened, how
  many days she wants, and what a fifth of thirty is. She says *thirty*, and *a day and a half*, and makes him say the
  whole of it back in the ordinary voice: **the name goes on it and the days go on it and the fifth comes off the days
  before it goes up and the person does not know what is left.** She asks her one question, standing, in daylight, and
  does not get it answered.
- **ch 674, day 2,077, *What The Levy Can Reach*. THE CLIMAX. IRREVERSIBLE: SHE IS ON A BOARD.** The twenty-fourth of the
  month beginning 2,054, **sixth hour to ninth hour — the three hours between them now carry a short passage the review
  repair added, and before it they did not.** **She holds one question back, asks it, and is told the answer: the name goes
  on it, the days go on it, the fifth comes off the days and not off the name, and the board on the first is a true
  statement about what went up.** Then she says her name out loud in daylight. He writes it in chalk in a county hand, in
  one line, without looking at it, and writes thirty days of the month beginning 2,084 beside it. **She does the sum out
  loud first: thirty days, one in twenty, a day and a half.** The woman of sixty walks down from the bench for the first
  time in nine years. **Nothing enormous happens and she says so plainly: it is a piece of wood four hundred yards from her
  hand, and it is the first time in her life there is a line in a hand anywhere in the world with her name at the end of
  it that is not in a book against her ribs.** It cannot be put back. She decides to stand in the grit at the first hour
  of the first of the month beginning 2,084, four feet back, where a cart can go past.
- **ch 675, days 2,078–2,084, *Counting The Days She Can See*. CLIMAX RESOLUTION.** Thirteen days at the frame and one
  trip up the mountain to the boy. **He counts the days to the first wrong — six — and she corrects him to three, because
  he counted off today and the first is not in the month that is written on the slate.** She comes down on the thirtieth,
  the day before, and says **one day is not enough for a mountain and four miles of made road if anything goes wrong, and
  it is enough if nothing goes wrong, and there is no second version of it.** **The board goes up on the first at the first
  hour on a cart with a piece of sacking over the slate, and eleven people go past the empty sockets in an hour and ten
  minutes and not one of them looks down.** The woman of sixty says the true thing — *it was not the writing, it was you
  wanting it* — and **she counts the eight firsts: 2,114 · 2,144 · 2,174 · 2,204 · 2,234 · 2,264 · 2,294**, and says that at
  the eighth of them something is going to happen that she cannot see from here. **The hook, said out loud: a board goes
  west up a road, and the thing that takes a fifth off a board does not go west, and there has been no fifth of her west
  of this turn since the eighteenth of the month beginning 1,814, and so the harvest is east, and she is going east in the
  morning with thirty days and not one single person who knows she is coming.** **The batch's last dated action is the
  ninth hour of the first of the month beginning 2,084, which is day 2,084** — this entry read *2,078–2,090* until the
  Batch 0001 review repair corrected it, and the same correction was made in `state/current.md`, `state/continuity.md` and
  `state/open-threads.md`.

## VOLUME 12 BATCH 0002 — ch 676–690, *the lamps*, drafted **and repaired after review**. Day 2,091 to day 2,140. Fifteen chapters, **28,894** words of body prose on method B, average 1,926. **The 28,804 and the 1,919 are the pre-repair page and must not be quoted.** *Re-measured per file by the Volume 12 planning review fix: **28,834 at average 1,922**. The 28,894 in this line does not reproduce against the files and is not to be quoted either.* *Corrected in place by the Volume 12 close, which repaired eight withdrawn mile figures in ch 676–689 (sixteen words added): **Batch 0002 is 28,850 at average 1,923, and 28,834 is struck.***

- **ch 676, day 2,091, the eighth of the month beginning 2,084, *The Bar Without A Strap*.** The six days are on the page and what they cost is: **the strap out of her own coat lining parts at the second hour of the fifth, six miles east of the turn, and after that she carries nine feet nine in her right hand for three days out of six, on the arm that is open four days in five — and the three days are seven miles, from six miles east of the turn to the thirteen.** Two miles and a bit a day. A count of fifty-four to get the bar off the ground at the gate on the seventh, and she says that figure out loud standing in the grit at the gate at the fourth hour, before she crosses the flat. **Nothing between the gate and the wall to lie down on, and it costs a shoulder.** She crosses the flat from that fourth hour, comes up to the wall at the second hour of the eighth and goes through it in the same hour, finds a fence that stops and stones that go on, goes into the rows for two hours and comes out. **She has five hundred and thirty-one days in her head and there is nowhere to put it and she does not say it, because a number said out loud in a ground with two hundred people in it is an announcement, and she cannot un-ask that.** Ends on the sixteenth day to the twenty-fourth and the twenty-three to the first of the month beginning 2,114.
- **ch 677, day 2,092, the ninth, *Every Cart Goes East*.** **Seven carts east and two west in an hour, one of the two empty.** Nobody is afraid of her and nobody is looking for her and she works out that people looked at her hand. She counts the road in her own paces, which is a bad unit and the only one she has. **At three miles past the wall the made road turns and there is a gate, a lane and a building with a lamp in every window at the seventh hour, standing in the open with no wall and forty people in front of it.** She says out loud that she is going to walk up to that gate in the morning and not one person in the world knows.
- **ch 678, day 2,093, the tenth, *The Name In The Book*.** Third row first: **the board at the north end went from two lines to six to four, and the boy tells her that and nothing else.** The lane, four carts under tarpaulins, and the first sight of the other end of a stair. **The man with the book looks up and says her name, and she does not answer, and not one other person in the room looks up** — which is what she paid for at the fourth hour of a month two years back. She works out that a person who is not on the roll costs the room nothing, so she is allowed to be in it. **She stays.**
- **ch 679, day 2,094, the eleventh, *What A Reader Is For*.** **The sheet read to her a second time in her life, six lines, same voice, forty-four years.** She asks one question — *what is it for* — and gets the answer nobody expected: **in forty-four years this room has never once stopped a thing going up a stair.** The trade is not protection and it is not payment. It is that a document gets read by somebody, and the reader is an instrument, and the moral answer she has been carrying for two years was the wrong question.
- **ch 680, day 2,095, the twelfth, *What Goes In The Book*.** She works out overnight that the sixth line is not a negotiation and that **the fifth line is the one the other four are for.** She says **no** first, out loud, in daylight, with the figure in her hand — **nineteen** — and every name in that book was nineteen when it went in. The man of sixty-five writes one line and the date in the ordinary hand and does not write why. **She does not make the entry read back to her this time.** And she keeps the second line in her head in daylight, because it goes up a stair.
- **ch 681, days 2,096–2,097, the thirteenth and fourteenth, *A Job Description*.** Four readers asked where they came from: **every one of them at nineteen out of a village, and not one of them was eighteen or twenty.** She works out that the sheet is a description of work and not an offer, and then she reads the first line properly — *confirmed in this room by another person in this room* — and works out that **to confirm a reading you have to be able to read it, so the hand that cannot be read in that room can never be confirmed, and she could never have been recruited, on any day, and has refused twice out loud for two years without knowing.**
- **ch 682, days 2,098–2,103, the fifteenth to the twentieth, *Furniture In A Room*.** Six days in a building where nothing is possible. **She costs the room nothing and the room has nothing to account for her, and a person with no rate, no room, no shelf and no limit on the call is furniture.** Her arm is open five days out of six. On the nineteenth a woman puts a sheet in front of her and does not ask and does not stop. The man with a shut notebook, nine feet off: **he has watched a person volunteer for a count, and the count was the only part anybody enjoyed.** She pays it — **she reads the sheets nobody in that room can read, for nothing, and there is going to be no line anywhere with her name on it that says she was here.**
- **ch 683, days 2,104–2,112, the twenty-first to the twenty-ninth, *The Line Between Two Numbers*. BATCH MIDPOINT.** A keeper’s sheet nailed to the wall at eye height for the twelve months ending on the twenty-seventh. **Total ninety-eight thousand; the seven lines come to ninety-five thousand and eighty; the difference is two thousand nine hundred and twenty, and eight coppers a day is a year of one person's bread.** She rules out every ordinary cause: both rate lines come out in whole pages, so **nobody in that room was paid short**; ninety-eight thousand is not a fifth of anything, so the levy is not in this building; the figure does not divide by thirty-six, so it did not come off the rate. **The line that is hers is the third rate — four coppers the page for a hand not one reader in this room can make out — six hundred and four coppers, a hundred and fifty-one pages.** She misses the twenty-fourth of the month beginning 2,084, the day the board is taken in, sixteen miles east. She carries the number six days and does not say it.
- **ch 684, day 2,113, the thirtieth, *Saying The Total*.** She says the whole thing out loud in daylight to about a hundred people, and **the room does arithmetic for ten minutes** — and a man of about sixty takes the sheet off the wall and four people work at it, and a reader of twenty-five asks her to do it again and it takes nine seconds. **One question: what is the money for. She is not permitted to say and has never been permitted to.** The man with the book: **there is no hand in this building that owns that line, and he looked this afternoon, before she said it.** She says the bill: she is nineteen and has nowhere to put a thing like that.
- **ch 685, days 2,114–2,128, the first to the fifteenth of the month beginning 2,114, *The Board At The North End*.** The second first passes and the board goes east without her. Fourteen days watching four men carry stones. **The interval is one hundred and thirty-one days and not seventy**, and the wrong figure came from counting off the day she could stand on (the turn, day 2,059): sixty-nine counted on her fingers came out as seventy, and the ninth stage is sixty-two days further back. **IMMORTAL VESSEL 1, at the fourth hour of the afternoon on the fifteenth, in the open, at the north end of the rows, in daylight, in front of about two hundred people, with no announcement. Corrected at the review: ch 686 and ch 689 had put it inside the Order's building and it was never there.** Its cost, named once: **she cannot be given a shape**, and it has to be made in her own hand. She writes one line on the board — her name, the date, the place, her age, and *I am not on a roll and I am not under a name above this ground and I am not going to be given one* — **with four fingers and a thumb, on an arm that was open.** The five blank leaves stay blank.
- **ch 686, days 2,129–2,130, the sixteenth and seventeenth, *Made By Hand*.** Three doors she knocked on: the book, a line on a sheet, and the people in the room. **The call is a rule about the book, and she stood at the counter with her hands on the wood for five hours on two days and was never called once.** She cannot announce herself because of the question she asked standing up on a floor, **and she could not have used the room either: the stage happened in the open two days before, not inside it.** **The finding: she is the first person in this ground who has gone up a stage with nothing under it** — the four men have a board, the readers a book, she has a slate at a turn and a line she wrote herself. **The board is four lines a night: four strokes by the first hour of the sixteenth, twelve by the first hour of the eighteenth, twenty by the eleventh hour of the twentieth.**
- **ch 687, day 2,131, the eighteenth, *A Name In Another Mouth*. REVERSAL.** A cart stops at the north end of the rows for the first time in nine years. **The man cannot read her line, and what comes out of his mouth is `YUAN SHENG`, twice, and there is no such name on any roll on that road.** She says one sentence and lets him go east. **He took Court at the first hour: he is addressable, and a thing above the line can put a name in a mouth he does not have, and a record with him in it can be read.** And she works out the cost of her own part: **he made himself findable and the thing that found him found the line she wrote three days ago, and she wrote the line.**
- **ch 688, days 2,132–2,133, the nineteenth and twentieth, *The Bill Left Unpaid*.** She spends a day counting how often a person in a ground of two hundred is looked for: **thirty-four times, by thirty-four different people, ordinary.** She raises the bill — one question, standing, in the dark — and it is not answered by anybody. **A thing that reads records has never wanted one in eleven hundred years and there is one now.** She works out that it will be priced in days, because days are the only thing she has ever had that was worth taking, and that the bill may land on a shape on a board and take the writing. On the twentieth a man in the fourth row asks whether her name is the one on the board, and a second cart stops and says a word and goes on.
- **ch 689, day 2,134, the twenty-first, *Letting Him Keep The Record*. IRREVERSIBLE, the batch's.** He asks for it standing, in daylight, at the north end of the rows, having never asked her for anything. **She says no first, properly, and then changes it, and says why, and says the ch 658 sentence out loud where a person can hear it: *for eight years I did not have a man, I had a column*.** Six terms, spoken in daylight in front of two hundred people: one at a time · in daylight · standing up · he says it out loud before it is a line anywhere else · a false line and she will know inside a day and there is no road back · **it is a record and not a hand.** He does not soften the cost. **The reason she said yes is true, and that is the part that is going to cost.**
- **ch 690, days 2,135–2,140, the twenty-second to the twenty-seventh, *Nothing Goes Wrong*.** Five days of it being true, said out loud, checked word by word, twice made to say a thing again. **The difficulty is that there is no page: the instrument is a voice, and a voice can be wrong, and nothing in nine days of road can settle it.** On the twenty-fifth she notices she has stopped checking, and does not remember deciding to. On the twenty-seventh he reads out loud the entry about the permission itself, and she says nothing at all, because there is no seventh term and a document agreed to in daylight cannot be added to either. **The first of the month beginning 2,144 is four days off.**


### VOLUME 12 BATCH 0002 — REVIEW REPAIR, one paragraph

`logs/volume-12-batch-0002.review.log` raised six hard errors and two false state claims. **All cleared; nothing structural
moved.** The stage was outdoors and three sentences had it indoors · **one event was being called four years old in some
places and five in others when it is two years and two months, 788 days from ch 474's day 1,306 to ch 679's day 2,094, and
twenty-eight sentences across seven chapters now agree** · **ch 690 claimed four false hands and an eleven-hundred-year false
line that are on no page and now claims ch 683's total instead** · **the board at the north end was counted as 13, then 25,
then 29, with five men writing where ch 685 says four take a turn; it now runs on four lines a night and lands 4 / 12 / 12 /
20 / six-nights / 48** · **ch 677's 1,406 stakes at twenty yards was sixteen miles and not nine, and the passage is rebuilt so
the arithmetic lands** · **ch 687 said four days where the fifteenth to the eighteenth is three.** Full record and the craft
measurements in `state/continuity.md` under ***VOLUME 12 BATCH 0002 — REVIEW REPAIR***.
